# Implementation blueprint, track D: port of the input-av input logic into the Core adapters

## 0. Summary

- **What this is.** The delegation plan for track D: everything that is built and closed on doubles on this Mac. 179 rows: 172 that run (unconditional, or conditional with the condition met: 7, 58, 59, 142), 4 still conditional on an experiment (23, 34, 56, 57), 2 skipped (9 unless row 141 reopens the branch selection; 173 by C21 (9)), and row 1, which is done in this document and not delegated. Rows 174 to 179 were added by the branch-B refinement (C23 to C27; `verification/05_security_branch_b.md`): branch B is selected, provisional until row 141, and its twelve binding requirements BR-01 to BR-12 are attached to their rows (section 11.4). Rows 161 to 173 are the re-cut of WP-13a on row 27's measurement and the controller's readings C18 (section 8.1). Rows 141 to 160 carry the requirements AD-01 to AD-14 of `verification/04_security_addendum.md` (CAUTION, 0 FAIL); step numbers are stable identifiers, so they follow row 140 in the table and sit in their milestone by their `[Mx]` prefix and their dependencies. No row needs a device, a Linux host, `sudo`, a real camera credential or the user.
- **State at 2026-10-13 05:35 (refresh after batch 6).** 189 rows: the 179 above, rows 180, 181, 182 and 186 that the controller added from findings, and rows 184, 185, 187, 188, 190 and 191 cut in this refresh (183 and 189 are not used). Done 79, in flight 14 (batch 7), skipped 2, open 94. Per row: the section `Row status at 2026-10-13 05:35` after the Execution Profile. Current totals, the path from here and the remaining duration: section 8.2. At that refresh the relay's own RTSP client waited for the user's ruling on C38; U17 has since ruled option (a) (section 9.2 condition 7). The counts of the first bullet are those of the branch-B refinement and are kept as written.
- **State at 2026-10-13 09:05 (extension for U15 to U19).** 233 rows: the 189 above, row 192 (it existed only in the brief of batch 8) and rows 193 to 235 cut in this extension. Done 98, in flight 16 (batch 8, with agent W of batch 7), skipped 2, open 117. New: milestone **M11, production boot for a bench test** (X-1 the production signing key, X-2 the operator passwords: rows 201 to 231, with SX-01 to SX-20 of `verification/07_security_x1_x2.md` attached, section 11.6); milestone **M12, real devices on this Mac, user-attended** (rows 232 to 235, bound by U18: search and connection test only); the Linux build of the worker (193); the re-address of a registered camera (194 to 198); the stored card identity (192, 199, 200). The user released the class-C lift (U15) and ruled the relay client (U17): every candidate mark is removed and the lift waits only for its engineering conditions. Per row: `Row status changes at 2026-10-13 09:05`; totals, path and duration: section 8.3. The sentence of the first bullet "no row needs ... the user" holds for every row except 233, which reads what the USER does at the web console; no row lets an agent type, generate for real use or read a real password or private key (section 6).
- **What it is not.** Track H (the run of the hardware acceptance test package, WP-15; the legacy data guard, WP-21) and stage 2 of the class-C lift ("fit to record") are not in this blueprint. They wait for the user and for the result of the package that rows 124 to 132 build.
- **Order.** The addendum's order: experiments first (E-11 before the relay), schema set A alone, adapters built in new files and proven at adapter level (T-1 to T-4), the lift in three parts with the first full session on doubles in WP-13c, commissioning behind its security review, the hardware package closed last.
- **Honesty of this document.** Sizes, models, token figures and durations are estimates. Paths marked `EXISTS:` were verified by Glob in this pass (list in section 14). New files are named only by their verified parent. Names of files that do not exist yet are the addendum's proposed names and are the executor's to change.

## 5. Scale-adjusted design

| Element | Choice |
|---|---|
| Research posture | None new: the research phase is closed. Three measuring rows (2, 27, and the first step of 89) settle what the addendum marks NOT VERIFIED |
| Specification depth | The addendum is the specification. Contract-landing rows go first and alone: 19 (audit vocabulary), 20 (adapter contract), 28 (selector contract), 38, 52, 63, 94, 102, 116 |
| Verification strategy | Unit and static checks in the row; one heavy scripted session per test row; a review and a gate per milestone; the full regression once (row 138) |
| Rollback | Each schema set is its own commit (C9) and is applied and rolled back once on the local database; every other milestone is one commit that reverts alone |

## 6. Row conventions (valid in every row)

- **Standing rules.** Nothing under `apps/input-av/data/` is opened. `apps/input-av/` is a read-only reference. Never `git clean`, `git stash`, `git add -A`, `git add .`; paths are staged by name; no push. No `sudo`, no host address change, no real camera, no real credential. Migrations only on this Mac's local PostgreSQL (the demo database and the throw-away databases of the suites), under `.locks/db-migrate.lock`. The capture fence files `boot.ts`, `operation.ts` and `checks.ts` under `apps/core-server/src/capture/` are not edited by any row except rows 80 and 81, which add host checks to `capture/boot/checks.ts`, and row 177 (row 58 before the branch-B split), which moves the worker version line there; none may change an existing refusal. Where `verification/04_security_addendum.md` corrects the addendum, the security check governs (section 11.3).
- **First act of every driver.** Declare the exact paths it will write (a claims file the controller reads). The Ownership Area of the profile is the bound; a path outside it is a request to the controller.
- **Tests in a row.** Targeted files only, `--maxWorkers=2`, one run if it passes and a second only after a failure. The unit suites (`npm test`) and `npm run test:db` are run once per commit by the controller, not by each row. `npm run test:db`, anything that binds a loopback port and anything that starts the capture worker need the command sandbox off. Anything that starts the capture worker, the relay or a scenario suite takes `.locks/heavy-host.lock`: one heavy job at a time on this host. New tests extend the test file of their subject; no per-wave test files.
- **Output paths.** Relative to this task folder. `implementation/NNN_*.md`, `test/NNN_*.md`, `gateway_check/NNN_*.md`, `verification/NNN_*.md`, `decisions/NNN_*.md`, `research/NNN_*.md` with NNN = the step number. The record names the change set; the code lives in the Ownership Area.
- **Prefixes.** `[Mx|WP-nn]` milestone and package; `[cond: ...]` runs only when the named outcome occurs, otherwise the controller marks it skipped with the outcome as evidence; `[derived]` not a part of the addendum's split, derived from the plan's phase goal.
- **Dependency kinds.** `B` = the code or the result of that row must exist. `C` = its specification, contract or decision text is enough. Waves are computed from `B` only. A milestone gate is never a `B` dependency of an implementation row, with two exceptions that are real security gates: row 8 (RC-1: E-11 before the relay) and row 86 (the security acceptance before the lift).
- **Narrative tiers.** T1 = full 5W1H (reviews, gates, decision drafts, procedure chapters); T2 = finding, why, how verified, source (experiments, measurements); T3 = statement plus evidence (change-set records, commits).
- **Hardware checks** are written `9A/H-nn` (finding F-10), because `H-nn` also names the 38 logic items of the research.
- **Secret scan (C23).** Wherever a row's verification cites the count-only scan (`scripts/secret-scan/count-only.mjs`, created by row 5), "the tree counts 0" is read as follows. Hard gate: 0 for both needles in every tracked log file, in this task's docs folder, and in everything a row of this task writes under `.cache`. The whole tracked tree is a baseline, not a gate: the camera-credential form counts 54 in 34 files (synthetic examples in tests, documents, the README, redaction sources); it may rise only by synthetic placeholders in test files, and the controller checks the delta by file name at every commit (the 13 commit rows); the ingest-secret form is 0 and stays 0 outside tests. This reading applies to the verification cell of row 5 as it landed and to row 137.
- **Branch B (C26, C27).** Branch B of addendum 5.3 is selected, provisional until row 141 (BR-10). A row marked `[branch B, BR-nn]` carries that binding requirement of `verification/05_security_branch_b.md`; the list is section 11.4. If row 141 reopens the selection, branch C applies without a further review, row 9 runs, and rows 58, 142 and 175 to 179 are what it replaces.
- **Secrets in M11 (U19, C42, C44; added by the extension of 2026-10-13 09:05).** No row lets an agent type, generate for real use, receive or read a real password or a real private key. Every password in a test is generated from randomness in that run and is no literal of a source file, fixture, log or record (SX-19); every key in a test is made into a fresh temporary directory that is removed with the run. The repository's `.data/` (its `signer` and `credential-store` among it), every `.env` and both database connection files are never opened, listed for content or written by a row. The terminal entry of the password command is never driven by an agent, by a pipe or by a pseudo-terminal: tests go through the command's function with an injected prompt port; the real prompt is typed by the user at a terminal with echo off. Rows of M11 use throw-away databases with the test name pattern, never the demo or a lane database (C29). The boot-check rule of the first bullet is extended by two rows only: row 211 (`capture/boot/index.ts`: one load, one exported predicate, no condition changed) and row 228 (`capture/boot/checks.ts`: added lines after check 4b); both are held to the baseline of row 205 (SX-17).
- **Real devices (U15, U16, U18, P11; the same extension).** The standing rule "no real camera, no real credential" stays for every row. Milestone M12 is the one exception, and it is the USER who acts: the user runs the camera search and the connection test in the web console and types the camera password there. An agent of M12 starts and stops a Core instance of this branch on a spare port with its own database and media store, and reads logs and results. No agent records from a real device, adopts a camera, changes a camera's address, signs in to the console or touches the user's microphone (on this Mac the production microphone path does not exist: it is ALSA on Linux).

**Rows added by the extension of 2026-10-13 09:05 (192 to 235).** Row 192 is written out as in flight; nothing of its task was changed against the brief. Where one finding or one work package of the design became more than one row, the reason is the size rule (one ownership area, at most three checks): the re-address is five rows (contract 194, shared action 195, engine door 196, wiring 197, dialog 198), as the microphone registrar was two (185, 190); the stored card identity is two (the module 199, the adapter 200); X-C is a vocabulary row and a schema row with its own commit and a mechanical pin row (201 to 204); the boot-unit edits of X1-3 and X2-3 are one row on one file (211) so that `capture/boot/index.ts` has one writer under one baseline; X2-3 is a probe, the command and its database acceptance (214 to 216); X1-4 is the resolver, the two commands, the retained set and a scenario (225, 226, 227, 229); X-D is three documents with three owners of record (217 to 219); X-G is a session, two reviews and the gate (220 to 223). M12 (a) is a start row and a stop row (232, 235) because no agent stays in flight across the user's own session. Rows 225 to 231 are M11's second tier: built in this task, not gating the bench gate (C42), gating real examinations (C44).

**Numbers not used (refresh of 2026-10-13).** 183 was never issued by the controller. 189 (the Linux first-open sample artefact and the silent-audio warning as open points of the hardware procedure) is not a row: row 125's chapter already carries both (`scripts/hardware-acceptance/HW-ACCEPTANCE-2-HOST-HELPER-AUDIO.md:466` states the bench finding and "a silent input is a `FAIL`", `:478` and `:488` are the first-second reading `9A/H-08.7` with its finding rule, `:504` bars a pass of `9A/H-17` with a silent track; the frame repeats it at `HW-ACCEPTANCE-1-FRAME.md:437`). Row 67 was in flight when this was checked and had no record yet; if its record adds a point the chapter lacks, it is an item for row 181. Rows 180, 181, 182 and 186 were added by the controller from findings and existed only in briefs until this refresh; rows 184, 185, 187, 188, 190 and 191 are cut here. Row 190 is the server half of the finding the controller numbered 185, and row 191 is the conditional build behind the measurement of row 184: each was split off because one row would have crossed two workspaces (185) or mixed a measurement with a build (184).

## 8. Waves, critical path, cost, duration

**Waves (build edges only).** 19 levels, 0 to 18. Level 0 holds 39 rows that have no build dependency: 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 11, 12, 13, 17, 19, 20, 24, 26, 27, 38, 39, 45, 52, 63, 65, 66, 76, 77, 80, 87, 94, 95, 103, 104, 109, 124, 125, 126, 128, 129, 135, 147, 160. That is the widest wave by dependencies. It will not run 39 wide. What limits it:

- the heavy-host lock: rows 6, 10, 12 and 39 of that level are heavy and run one after another;
- single writers: `static-capture` (26, 65, 66, 80 queue behind 21), `op-pins` (63 alone), the migration writer (17 alone), `static-adapters` (147 before 45);
- the controller's mediation: the width measured on this host in task 3 was 12 to 14 drivers with targeted tests (CLAUDE.md section 6). Expected practical width of the first waves: about 10 to 14.

**Rows that may start before the previous gate.** Every implementation row. Gates block sign-off and commits, not coding, except row 8 (before rows 41, 47, 58, 142) and row 86 (before row 88).

**Critical path** (longest chain of build edges): 6 -> 7 -> 8 -> 41 -> 42 -> 43 -> 153 -> 141 -> 86 -> 88 -> 89 -> 90 -> 151 -> 91 -> 92 -> 93 -> 138 -> 139 -> 140. In sizes: M S S M M M M M M M M M M M S S M M S. It is the camera path, as in the plan, lengthened by the security gates (AD-01 (6) puts the container repeat of E-11 before the lift). The commissioning chain (17 -> 99 -> 100 -> 102 -> 105 -> 106 -> 107 -> 111 -> 119 -> 121) ends at level 8 and is no longer co-critical.

What would shorten it: (1) E-11 is the first heavy job of the run, with the test camera (39, 40, 152) queued directly behind it, so the relay rows wait for nothing but the gate; (2) if no observation is ACCEPTED, row 7 is skipped; (3) row 141 could be cut in two so that the E-11 repeat needs only row 78 and not the relay confinement of row 153: one level less, at the cost of a second container session; (4) row 85 and the audio rows run beside the relay rows, so the lift waits only for row 86.

**Critical path after the branch-B refinement.** Not longer: 19 levels, row 141 stays at level 7. A second chain of the same length now reaches it: 6 -> 7 -> 8 -> 58 -> 175 -> 176 -> 178 -> 141 (sizes M S S M S S M M; rows 6 and 7 have run), beside 8 -> 41 -> 42 -> 43 -> 153 -> 141. Row 142 (level 5, after 175) and row 177 (level 6) are off it; row 59 is at level 7 and is a build dependency of row 86 (level 8), which it does not move. Both chains build the worker folder under one builder and the heavy-host lock, so they do not run side by side: the levels do not move, the wall-clock does (duration below). What would shorten it: the worker chain starts the moment this refinement is accepted, since row 8's inputs exist (C27), while the relay chain still waits for the test camera (39, 40); and row 141 cut in two (decision 10 of section 13), so that the announce-line half needs rows 78, 142 and 178 and not the relay confinement of row 153.

**Integration passes (real work the table does not show).** At each quiet point (no code row in flight) the controller launches one integration pass: one small agent, sole code writer while it runs, with a fixed list taken from `delegation_progress/file_requests.md` (barrel exports, pins outside a row's area, one-line follows of a widened type, the generated description regenerated once). I-1 is done (in commit 7969f0fa). I-2 is pending; among its items are the four one-line follows of the nullable `activeInputId` outside the file map of WP-13a (register of row 161, section 9, R-2) once row 28 has landed. A pass has no row number; its record is `delegation_progress/integration_iNN.md`, and its cost is inside the allowance for gates and fix waves.

**The three most expensive rows (estimate).** By tokens: 88 (the lift change set with 21 pin rows across six files), 41 (the relay in C with Digest-only and the library pin), 89 (the first full session with a real key maker and four doubles). By wall-clock: 138 (the full regression, about 75 minutes by the README, plus the new suites), 59 if it runs (the worker series, about 35 minutes, plus the kill matrix, about 10), 12 (browser, emulator and export in one session).

**Cost statement (estimate, not a measurement).**

| Measure | Count |
|---|---|
| Rows | 179 (172 that run, 4 still conditional: 23, 34, 56, 57; 2 skipped: 9 unless reopened, 173; 1 not delegated); 173 before the branch-B refinement, 160 before the WP-13a refinement |
| By size | S 107, M 72, L 0 (before the branch-B refinement: S 105, M 68; rows 28 and 142 went from S to M, 4 new S: 174 to 177, 2 new M: 178, 179) |
| By model | opus 160, sonnet 18 (rows 2, 4, 13, 160, 177 and the 13 commit rows), not delegated 1 |
| By agent | coder-execution 104, test-expert 22, git-specialist 14, verifier 10, document-writer 7, senior-code-reviewer 7, data-analyst 7, database-specialist 3, security-review 3, task-architect 2 |
| Waves | 19; widest by dependencies 39 (level 0, unchanged), practical width about 10 to 14 |
| Token budget | S at 60 to 150 thousand, M at 200 to 350 thousand: about 21 to 42 million tokens for all 179 rows (the branch-B refinement adds about 0.9 to 1.7 million to the 20 to 40 of the 173 rows: 4 S, 2 M and two steps from S to M); fix waves and integration passes are not in it |

**Revised duration of track D (estimate, not a commitment, no calendar date).** The plan's figure was 11 to 16.5 working days before the splits (`masterplan/02_plan.md` section 21.3); the addendum added 3 to 4 (its section 11), which gives 14 to 20.5 by the package rate. Two bases from this blueprint:

| Basis | Derivation | Working days |
|---|---|---|
| Row pace | P8's measured pace of task 3: more than 100 rows in 6 days, about 17 rows a day with parallel work. 150 unconditional rows / 17 | about 9 |
| Wave count | 19 levels; task 3 closed 2 to 3 levels a day; the 35 heavy rows are serialised by one lock | 6.5 to 9.5 |
| Package rate (ceiling) | the plan's unit rate with the addendum's additions, plus the 20 security rows (16 S, 4 M) at the same rate | 15 to 22 |

Taken together: **about 10 to 16 working days before gates and fix waves; about 14 to 24 with the 40 to 50 % that P8 allows for them.** Branch C of E-11 adds the 3 to 5 days of addendum 5.4 and a refinement pass. The lower end rests on the row pace of task 3 carrying over to rows that are, on average, smaller than the plan's packages; that is an assumption.

**Change by the WP-13a refinement (estimate).** The three bases above are left as they were computed for 160 rows. The re-cut adds 12 unconditional rows (10 S, 2 M) and one conditional S row, while row 28 steps down from M to S: by row pace 12 / 17 = about 0.7 working day; by package rate, at the rate the 20 security rows were counted with, about 0.7 to 1; by wave count nothing, because the critical path does not move (section 8.1). **The estimate rises by about 0.7 to 1 working day: about 11 to 17 working days before gates and fix waves, about 15 to 25 with the allowance.** Gate M2 closes two levels later than before (level 8 instead of 6).

**Change by the branch-B refinement (estimate).** The bases above are left as computed. What is added to the work that certainly runs: 6 new rows (174 to 179) and 3 rows that were conditional and now run (58, 59, 142); row 7 has already run; rows 9 and 173 are skipped. By row pace 9 / 17 = about 0.5 working day. By the heavy-host lock: six worker rows (58, 175, 176, 177, 142, 178), each a worker build with a targeted run, the re-verification of row 59 (about 45 minutes) and the longer container session of row 141 queue with the relay rows under one builder: about 3 to 5 hours of serialised host time, part of which overlaps the row-pace figure. **The estimate rises by about 0.6 to 1 working day; of that, BR-05 (row 176 and its case in row 178) is about 0.1 to 0.2. New range: about 12 to 18 working days before gates and fix waves, about 16 to 26 with the allowance.** Branch C would have added the 3 to 5 days of addendum 5.4; that figure applies again only if row 141 reopens the selection. By wave count nothing changes (19 levels).

## 10. Verification and testing strategy

- **Unit and static tests:** in the producing row, targeted files, written by the row's agent; coverage goal: every acceptance check of the row is a test that fails without the change.
- **Integration (adapter level):** T-1 to T-3 in rows 46 and 67; T-2 for audio in row 69; T-4 in rows 78, 79, 141. No registry, fence or session before the lift.
- **Functional (full session on doubles):** rows 89, 90, 148, 151 after the lift, on clone databases of the suite that hold the seeded bulk data plus the production rows the test writes; never on the demo database.
- **Database:** `npm run test:db` after each schema set, with pins re-measured from the generated file; each set applied and rolled back once on the demo database.
- **Security:** row 86 before the lift, row 119 on the commissioning functions, row 137 at the end; E-12 extended (44), the hygiene run (159), the secret search (151).
- **Performance and risk:** the connection test inside 15,000 ms (50), the open bound of 25,000 ms and the window of 30,000 ms on fake clocks (46, 47, 90); real figures are `9A/H-21`.
- **Rollback and recovery:** per schema set its rollback run (17, 99, 114); per milestone one commit that reverts alone; the lift reverts as one change set and leaves sealed sessions valid.
- **UAT:** not in track D. It is the hardware package, run by the user.
- **Bulk data seeding:** the existing seed of the Core; no new seed script. The demo database keeps its seeded content through each apply and rollback.
- **Regression:** the suites named in the README once per commit by the controller; `npm run test:regression` once in row 138 (it does not run the real-engine transcriber tests and opens no device).
- **Acceptance of track D:** rows 136 to 139 pass and the controller's report says "done on doubles" with the list of section 12.
- **M11 (added by the extension of 2026-10-13 09:05):** unit tests in the producing rows with injected ports (prompt, uid, stat, clock); the real boot function as the judge (208, 216, 220); a probe before the password command is written (214); database acceptance on throw-away databases with the test name pattern (216, 220, 229); the sweeps for key material and for run-generated passwords at 0 (206, 216, 220); the count-only scan with the needles of row 207; the baseline comparison by symbol (205 against 211, 223, 228); two security reviews as built (221, 230). Every check runs on this Mac. What an agent cannot test is the real prompt with a real person: one attended check by the user on macOS and on Ubuntu 24.04 (section 12).
- **M12 (the same extension):** not a test of the product: an observation of the user's own search and connection test, read from logs and results (233), with counts that nothing was recorded, adopted or re-addressed, and a classification of every difference from the doubles (234).

**X-1 and X-2: superseded by U19 (extension of 2026-10-13 09:05).** Both are now built in this task as milestone M11 (rows 201 to 231), so the 21 hardware checks named below no longer stop for want of a command: they wait for the user to run the two commands on the acceptance host, and the frame's passages are rewritten by row 219. The paragraph that follows is the reading of the 05:35 refresh, kept as a record; its last sentence ("no row is added") no longer holds, and decision 16 is ruled. **As read at 05:35:** X-1 and X-2 were outside this blueprint, and the hardware procedure stopped without them. X-1 is the provisioning of the production signing key; X-2 is a means to give every operator a password that is not the published demonstration password. The plan lists both as the user's external actions, and row 124 records the consequence: no command of the repository provisions a production key, the product has no password-change function, and the frame says so (`scripts/hardware-acceptance/HW-ACCEPTANCE-1-FRAME.md:101-102`, `:257`, `:451`). Without either of them `9A/H-16`, the first production boot at sequence 10 of the frame's order, is recorded "not run", and every check after it is "not run" for the same reason. Of the 27 hardware checks, 6 can run without X-1 and X-2: `9A/H-27`, `H-01`, `H-02`, `H-24`, `H-03`, `H-04` (sequences 2 to 8, none of them in `REAL_PRODUCTION`). The other 21 cannot: `9A/H-16` itself and `9A/H-05` to `H-15`, `H-17` to `H-23`, `H-25` and `H-26`, among them every check with a real camera, a real sound card or the installed helper, and the first session from four production sources. No row is added for X-1 or X-2: a signing-key provisioning command or an operator-password tool would be new product scope, and whether the port builds either, or the user provides them by other means before the day, is the user's decision (decision 16 of section 13).

## 13. Risk assessment, recommendation and next plan

### Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| The lift's critical path is long and serialised by one heavy-host lock | E-11 first; fixtures queued behind it; option (3) of section 8 if the container session becomes the bottleneck |
| No container runtime on this Mac | row 78 reports it as its first check; decision 1 |
| Digest-only cannot be held by the pinned library | row 41 returns `blocked`; a stop for the user (addendum section 10 item 4) |
| A row grows beyond its size during execution | the driver returns `needs-review` with what is not built; the finding becomes a new S or M row, never a clause |
| The worker's log file shows the ingest secret (HX-3) | row 151 finds it; then the worker's callback changes, row 59 runs, and "unchanged worker" no longer holds: a logged decision |
| Linux-only code is developed on macOS (plan R-22) | seams, text equality, the container rows; everything else is section 12 |
| The worker does not build on Linux today (row 78: five causes) and a sixth may appear | row 193 before rows 79 and 141; a cause beyond the five returns `needs-review` and becomes its own row; until it lands the branch-B gate on the Linux library is unproven (section 12) |
| An agent supplies or sees a real secret to make an M11 test or gate pass | section 6, "Secrets in M11": injected prompt port, run-generated values, temporary directories, no `.data/`, no connection file; the terminal entry refuses a pipe; rows 221 and 230 state it from the records or refuse to |
| The password command's audit row cannot be written through the existing writer from a host command (H2) | row 214 answers before row 215 starts; on "no" X-2 stops for a new review (SX-02), and X-1 and the rotation tier go on |
| A real camera's password reaches a log during M12 | the user types it in the web console only; no agent signs in; decision 24 (row 44 first); the scan of row 233 with its stated limit |
| The bench gate is read as "fit to record" | gate 223 and the README (row 218) quote list A as open; decision 22 is put to the user |

## 15. Agent mapping rationale

- **coder-execution:** every change to product or test-support code. Opus throughout except rows 13 and 177 (the version rise: two specified lines): the rows carry security, concurrency or evidence-integrity judgment.
- **database-specialist:** the three schema sets, each with its migration, pins and rollback as one unit.
- **test-expert:** experiments, heavy scripted sessions and the full regression; it writes the test files of its own rows. Never downgraded.
- **verifier, security-review, senior-code-reviewer:** gates, the three security reviews, the per-milestone and final code reviews. Never downgraded.
- **document-writer:** the host profile, the four chapters of the hardware procedure, the README, the installer note.
- **data-analyst:** measurements and decision-row drafts that the controller then records.
- **git-specialist:** the guard check and the 13 commits, mechanical, sonnet.
- **task-architect:** this document; row 9 only in branch C.
- **Extension of 2026-10-13 09:05.** coder-execution builds the two commands, the prompt, the policy, the resolver and the re-address (opus: each holds a security or evidence-integrity judgment); sonnet only for row 204 (pin lines, search-verifiable) and row 235 (stop listed processes and report). database-specialist owns the schema step X-C as it owns the three sets. test-expert owns the probe before the password command (214), the two acceptance sessions (216, 220), the rotation scenario (229), the X-1 proof (208) and both M12 rows that run or read an instance (232, 233); it is never downgraded. security-review rates X-1 and X-2 as built twice (221, 230), because the design's review saw no code. git-specialist takes the three commits and the baseline record of row 205, which is two digests and four quotations from a commit. data-analyst turns the real-device observations into owned findings (234). document-writer revises three documents that state a limit this task now closes (217, 218, 219).
